figure 2: this figure is related to algorithm 5.

definition 8.3 ( $\eta, \theta, \alpha, \beta$  and  $\gamma$ ). we define

$$
\eta_ {i, s} = \sqrt {z _ {0} - x _ {0}} \cdot q (y _ {s, 0}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i}
$$

$$
\theta_ {i, t} = \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot q (z _ {t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

$$
\alpha_ {i, s, t} = \sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}} \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

$$
\beta_ {i, s, t} = \sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}} \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) \cdot q (z _ {t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

$$
\gamma_ {i, s, t} = \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot (z _ {t} - x _ {t}) \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i}.
$$

here we make the simplifying assumption that  $(z_{t} - x_{t})$  is psd for every  $t\in [0,1]$  and that  $(w^{\mathrm{mid}} - w^{\mathrm{new}})$  is psd. in the case where these might not hold, we can simply break these matrices into positive and negative parts and bound both parts using essentially the same calculations.

lemma 8.4 (leverage score's moving). the change in leverage score can be written as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - \sigma (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} = \int_ {0} ^ {1} \theta_ {i, t} ^ {\top} \theta_ {i, t} \mathrm {d} t + 2 \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \alpha_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \beta_ {i, s, t} \mathrm {d} s \mathrm {d} t + \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \gamma_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \gamma_ {i, s ^ {\prime}, t} \mathrm {d} s \mathrm {d} s ^ {\prime} \mathrm {d} t \\ + \tau \left(v ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) _ {i} \cdot \left(w _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \eta_ {i, s} ^ {\top} \eta_ {i, s} \mathrm {d} s. \tag {12} \\ \end{array}
$$

proof. we first rewrite  $\sigma(w^{\mathrm{new}})_i - \sigma(w^{\mathrm{mid}})_i$  into two terms

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - \sigma (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} = w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \\ = w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \cdot (\tau (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i}) + \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

the lemma then follows immediately from the following lemma 8.5 and 8.6.

lemma 8.5 (first term in leverage score's moving). for the first term in lemma 8.4, we have

$$
w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \cdot (\tau (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i}) = \int_ {0} ^ {1} \theta_ {i, t} ^ {\top} \theta_ {i, t} \mathrm {d} t + 2 \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \alpha_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \beta_ {i, s, t} \mathrm {d} s \mathrm {d} t + \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \gamma_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \gamma_ {i, s ^ {\prime}, t} \mathrm {d} s \mathrm {d} s ^ {\prime} \mathrm {d} t.
$$

proof. we can compute  $\tau(w^{\mathrm{new}})_i - \tau(w^{\mathrm{mid}})_i$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \\ = \left(a \left(a ^ {\top} w ^ {\text {n e w}} a\right) ^ {- 1} a ^ {\top}\right) _ {i, i} - \left(a \left(a ^ {\top} w ^ {\text {m i d}} a\right) ^ {- 1} a ^ {\top}\right) _ {i, i} \\ = \left[ \int_ {0} ^ {1} a \left(a ^ {\top} \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} + t \left(w ^ {\text {n e w}} - w ^ {\text {m i d}}\right)\right) a\right) ^ {- 1} a ^ {\top} \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) a \left(a ^ {\top} \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} + t \left(w ^ {\text {n e w}} - w ^ {\text {m i d}}\right)\right) a\right) ^ {- 1} a ^ {\top} \mathrm {d} t \right] _ {i, i} \\ = \left[ \int_ {0} ^ {1} q (x _ {t}) \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) q (x _ {t}) \mathrm {d} t \right] _ {i, i}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last step follows by the definition of  $q(v) = a(a^{\top}va)^{-1}a^{\top} \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times m}$  and  $x_{t} = w^{\mathrm{mid}} + t(w^{\mathrm{new}} - w^{\mathrm{mid}})$ . now we write  $q(x_{t}) - q(z_{t})$  in a similar way as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} q (x _ {t}) - q (z _ {t}) = a \left(a ^ {\top} x _ {t} a\right) ^ {- 1} a ^ {\top} - a \left(a ^ {\top} z _ {t} a\right) ^ {- 1} a ^ {\top} \\ = \int_ {0} ^ {1} q \left(y _ {s, t}\right) \cdot \left(z _ {t} - x _ {t}\right) \cdot q \left(y _ {s, t}\right) d s, \\ \end{array}
$$

where recall the definition of  $y_{s,t} = z_t + s(x_t - z_t)$ . using the above expressions, we can rewrite  $w_i^{\mathrm{new}} \cdot (\tau(w^{\mathrm{new}})_i - \tau(w^{\mathrm{mid}})_i)$  as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \cdot (\tau (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {i} - \tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i}) \\ = w _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} \cdot \left[ \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left(q (z _ {t}) + \int_ {0} ^ {1} q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \left(z _ {t} - x _ {t}\right) \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \mathrm {d} s\right) \cdot \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) \right. \\ \left. \cdot \left(q (z _ {t}) + \int_ {0} ^ {1} q \left(y _ {s ^ {\prime}, t}\right) \cdot \left(z _ {t} - x _ {t}\right) \cdot q \left(y _ {s ^ {\prime}, t}\right) \mathrm {d} s ^ {\prime}\right) \mathrm {d} t \right] _ {i, i} \\ = \int_ {0} ^ {1} e _ {i} ^ {\top} \sqrt {w ^ {\text {n e w}}} \cdot q (z _ {t}) \cdot \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) \cdot q (z _ {t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\text {n e w}}} e _ {i} \mathrm {d} t \\ + 2 \int_ {0} ^ {1} e _ {i} ^ {\top} \sqrt {w ^ {\text {n e w}}} \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot (z _ {t} - x _ {t}) \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \mathrm {d} s\right) (w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}) q (z _ {t}) \sqrt {w ^ {\text {n e w}}} e _ {i} \mathrm {d} t \\ + \int_ {0} ^ {1} e _ {i} ^ {\top} \sqrt {w ^ {\text {n e w}}} \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \left(z _ {t} - x _ {t}\right) \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) d s\right) \cdot \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}\right) \\ \cdot \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} q \left(y _ {s ^ {\prime}, t}\right) \cdot \left(z _ {t} - x _ {t}\right) \cdot q \left(y _ {s ^ {\prime}, t}\right) \mathrm {d} s ^ {\prime}\right) \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} e _ {i} \mathrm {d} t \\ = \int_ {0} ^ {1} \theta_ {i, t} ^ {\top} \theta_ {i, t} d t + 2 \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \alpha_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \beta_ {i, s, t} d s d t + \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \gamma_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \gamma_ {i, s ^ {\prime}, t} d s d s ^ {\prime} d t. \\ \end{array}
$$

lemma 8.6 (second term in leverage score's moving). for the second term in lemma 8.4, we have

$$
\tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) = \tau (v ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \eta_ {i, s} ^ {\top} \eta_ {i, s} \mathrm {d} s.
$$

proof. we can rewrite  $\tau(w^{\mathrm{mid}})_i \cdot (w_i^{\mathrm{new}} - w_i^{\mathrm{mid}})$  as

$$
\tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) = \tau (v ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) + (\tau (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} - \tau (v ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i}) \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}).
$$

the second term can be computed as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(w _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) \cdot \left(\tau \left(w ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) _ {i} - \tau \left(v ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) _ {i}\right) \\ = \left(w _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) \cdot \left(\tau \left(x _ {0}\right) _ {i} - \tau \left(z _ {0}\right) _ {i}\right) \\ = \left(w _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\text {m i d}}\right) \cdot \left[ \int_ {0} ^ {1} q \left(z _ {0} + s \left(x _ {0} - z _ {0}\right)\right) \cdot \left(z _ {0} - x _ {0}\right) \cdot q \left(z _ {0} + s \left(x _ {0} - z _ {0}\right)\right) \mathrm {d} s \right] _ {i, i} \\ = - \int_ {0} ^ {1} e _ {i} ^ {\top} \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot q (y _ {s, 0}) \cdot (z _ {0} - x _ {0}) \cdot q (y _ {s, 0}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i} \mathrm {d} s \\ = - \int_ {0} ^ {1} \eta_ {i, s} ^ {\top} \eta_ {i, s} d s. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/b56dd5ebb954bccd4859951a4ca959bbc06320b5fb591331f103bc73936768ef.jpg)

# 8.3 running time anlaysis

lemma 8.7 (update time). assume the sequence acts satisfies assumption 7.1 and  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$ . then each call to the function update(acts) makes  $o(n)$  calls to pm.update and takes an extra  $o(n^{2} \cdot |\mathsf{acts}| + \mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n, n, r) \cdot n^{3} \cdot \log(n))$  time.

proof. the number of calls to pm.update directly follows from algorithm 5. the time to compute  $\delta \widetilde{\sigma}^{\mathrm{mid}}$  is  $o(n^{2}\cdot |\mathsf{acts}|)$  by applying theorem 6.3. the remaining part of the running time is given by the following lemma 8.8, 8.9 and 8.10.

lemma 8.8 (computing  $\theta^{\top}\theta$ ). assume  $m = o(n)$ . then for any  $t \in \mathcal{t}$ , the time to compute  $\theta_{i,t}^{\top}\theta_{i,t}$  for all  $i \in [m]$  is  $o(n^{2})$ .

proof. recall from definition 8.3 that

$$
\theta_ {i, t} = \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot q (z _ {t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i}.
$$

denote  $q = q(z_{t})$  and  $q^{(2)}$  the entry-wise square of  $q$ . we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \theta_ {i, t} ^ {\top} \theta_ {i, t} = w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} e _ {i} ^ {\top} q (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) q e _ {i} \\ = w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \cdot \sum_ {j \in [ m ]} (q _ {i, j}) ^ {2} (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) _ {j} \\ = w _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} \cdot e _ {i} ^ {\top} q ^ {(2)} (w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore, in order to compute  $\theta_{i,t}^{\top}\theta_{i,t}$  for all  $i\in [m]$ , it suffices to compute the matrix-vector product  $q^{(2)}(w^{\mathrm{mid}} - w^{\mathrm{new}})$ , which takes time  $o(n^{2})$ .

![](images/f9542e984ed905e8403955185e09bd888a6792ee432b730bb5d9ed5077c005b9.jpg)

lemma 8.9 (computing  $\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \eta$ ). assume  $m = o(n)$  and  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$ . for any  $r \geq 0$ ,  $t \in \mathcal{t}$ ,  $s, s' \in s$  and matrices  $r_{\alpha \beta, s, t}, r_{\gamma, s, s', t}, r_{\eta, s} \in \mathbb{r}^{r \times m}$ , the time to compute  $r_{\alpha \beta, s, t} \alpha_{i, s, t}$ ,  $r_{\alpha \beta, s, t} \beta_{i, s, t}$ ,  $r_{\gamma, s, s', t} \gamma_{i, s, t}$ ,  $r_{\gamma, s, s', t} \gamma_{i, s', t}$ ,  $r_{\eta, s} \eta_{i, s}$  for all  $i \in [m]$  (up to negligible error) is  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n, n, r) \cdot \log(n))$  using fast matrix multiplication.

proof. we only describe how to compute  $r_{\alpha \beta, s, t} \alpha_{i, s, t}$  for all  $i \in [m]$  in time  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n, n, r) \cdot \log(n))$  as the rest of the calculations are similar. notice that computing  $r_{\alpha \beta, s, t} \alpha_{i, s, t}$  for all  $i \in [m]$  is essentially computing the matrix

$$
\{r _ {\alpha \beta , s, t} \alpha_ {i, s, t} \} _ {i \in [ m ]} = r _ {\alpha \beta , s, t} \sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}} \cdot a \cdot m (y _ {s, t}) ^ {- 1} \cdot a ^ {\top} \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}.
$$

assume we know the matrix  $m(y_{s,t})^{-1}$ , then we can perform the computation from left to right, and it follows that each matrix multiplication here can be done in time  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,r))$ . to remove the assumption that we know  $m(y_{s,t})^{-1}$ , we pre-condition on the matrix  $m(z_{t})^{-1}$  and apply lemma 6.1 to compute  $r_{\alpha \beta ,s,t}\alpha_{i,s,t}$  for all  $i\in [m]$  (up to negligible error) in time  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,r)\cdot \log (n))$ .

lemma 8.10 (computing  $\delta \widetilde{\sigma}^{\mathrm{new}}$ ). assume  $m = o(n)$  and  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$ . define an approximation  $\delta \widetilde{\sigma}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  to the change in leverage score to be

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta \widetilde {\sigma} _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} = \tau (v ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) _ {i} \cdot (w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - w _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) + \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \omega_ {t} \theta_ {i, t} ^ {\top} \theta_ {i, t} - \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {s} \eta_ {i, s} ^ {\top} r _ {\eta , s} ^ {\top} r _ {\eta , s} \eta_ {i, s} \\ + 2 \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} \omega_ {s} \alpha_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\alpha \beta , s, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\alpha \beta , s, t} \beta_ {i, s, t} + \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \sum_ {s ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} \omega_ {s} \omega_ {s ^ {\prime}} \gamma_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\gamma , s, s ^ {\prime}, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\gamma , s, s ^ {\prime}, t} \gamma_ {i, s ^ {\prime}, t}. \\ \end{array}
$$

then the time to compute  $\delta \widetilde{\sigma}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  for all  $i\in [m]$  (up to negligible error) is  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,r)\cdot n^3\cdot \log (n))$

proof. since we know  $\tau(v^{\mathrm{mid}})_i = q(v^{\mathrm{mid}})_{i,i}$  and  $w_i^{\mathrm{new}} - w_i^{\mathrm{mid}}$ , we can compute the first term for all  $i \in [m]$  in  $o(n)$  time. by lemma 8.8, the time to compute the second term for all  $i \in [m]$  is  $o(n^2 \cdot n)$ . it follows from lemma 8.9 that the time to compute the rest of the terms for all  $i \in [m]$  is  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,r) \cdot n^3 \cdot \log(n))$ .

# 8.4 upper bounding  $\eta, \alpha, \beta$  and  $\gamma$

from the computation in section 8.5, the variance of the three terms where we applied jl matrices are bounded by  $\sum_{i}\| \eta_{i}\|_{2}^{4}$ ,  $\sum_{i}\| \alpha_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\| \beta_{i}\|_{2}^{2}$  and  $\sum_{i}\| \gamma_{i}\|_{2}^{4}$ . our bounds for these terms are summarized in table 6.

<table><tr><td>quantity</td><td>bound</td><td>lemma</td></tr><tr><td>∑m i=1n ||ηi||2/2</td><td>εcomp2 · ||log(wnew) - log(wmid)||2/2</td><td>lemma 8.11</td></tr><tr><td>∑m i=1n ||αi||2/2 ||βi||2/2</td><td>εcomp2 · ||log(wnew) - log(wmid)||2/2</td><td>lemma 8.12</td></tr><tr><td>∑m i=1n ||γi||2/2</td><td>εcomp4 · ||log(wnew) - log(wmid)||2/2</td><td>lemma 8.13</td></tr></table>

table 6: upper bounds for  $\sum_{i} \| \eta_{i} \|_{2}^{4}$ ,  $\sum_{i} \| \alpha_{i} \|_{2}^{2} \| \beta_{i} \|_{2}^{2}$  and  $\sum_{i} \| \gamma_{i} \|_{2}^{4}$ , where  $\eta, \alpha, \beta$  and  $\gamma$  are defined in definition 8.3.

# 8.4.1 upper bounding  $\sum_{i=1}^{m} \|\eta_i\|_2^4$

lemma 8.11. assume  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$ , where  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}}$  is the error parameter in algorithm 5. then for any  $s \in s$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \| \eta_ {i, s} \| _ {2} ^ {4} = o (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}} ^ {2}) \cdot \left\| \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. we recall the definition of vector  $\eta_{i,s} \in \mathbb{r}^m$  from definition 8.3 as follows:

$$
\eta_ {i, s} = \sqrt {z _ {0} - x _ {0}} \cdot q (y _ {s, 0}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

where  $y_{s,t} = z_t + s(x_t - z_t)$  and  $y_{s,t} = z_t + s(x_t - z_t)$ . recall from definition 3.2 that  $p(v) = v^{1/2}a(a^\top va)^{-1}a^\top v^{1/2} = v^{1/2} \cdot q(v) \cdot v^{1/2}$ . we can rewrite  $\eta_{i,s}$  as

$$
\eta_ {i, s} = \frac {\sqrt {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} p (y _ {s, 0}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} e _ {i}.
$$

therefore, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \eta_ {i, s} \right\| _ {2} ^ {4} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left(e _ {i} ^ {\top} \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} p (y _ {s, 0}) \frac {\sqrt {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} \cdot \frac {\sqrt {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} p (y _ {s, 0}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} e _ {i}\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} p \left(y _ {s, 0}\right) \frac {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}{y _ {s , 0}} p \left(y _ {s, 0}\right) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left[ \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} p \left(y _ {s, 0}\right) \frac {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}{y _ {s , 0}} p \left(y _ {s, 0}\right) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} \right. \\ . \left. \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} p (y _ {s, 0}) \frac {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}{y _ {s , 0}} p (y _ {s, 0}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , 0}}} \right] \\ = \operatorname {t r} [ (\sqrt {b} p c p \sqrt {b}) ^ {2} ], \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we define diagonal matrices  $b, c \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times m}$  as

$$
b = \frac {w ^ {\text {m i d}} - w ^ {\text {n e w}}}{y _ {s , 0}}, c = \frac {z _ {0} - x _ {0}}{y _ {s , 0}},
$$

and for simplicity, we use  $p$  to denote the projection matrix  $p(y_{s,0})$ . since  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$ , it follows that

$$
\left\| \log \left(y _ {s, 0}\right) - \log \left(x _ {0}\right) \right\| _ {\infty} \leq 0. 0 1.
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {t r} \left[ (\sqrt {b} p c p \sqrt {b}) ^ {2} \right] \\ \begin{array}{l l} \leq \| c \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \cdot \operatorname {t r} [ b ^ {2} ] & p \cdot c \cdot p \preceq \| c \| _ {\infty} \cdot i \\ = \| c \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \cdot \| b \| _ {2} ^ {2} \end{array} \\ = o (1) \cdot \| \log (z _ {0}) - \log (x _ {0}) \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \log (w ^ {\text {n e w}}) - \log (w ^ {\text {m i d}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \left\| \log \left(w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}\right) - \log \left(w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/ee58e3d53ebc160c474694d76aa020c39c44b17897d679253587e1c1b961cb7b.jpg)

# 8.4.2 upper bounding  $\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|\alpha_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\beta_{i}\|_{2}^{2}$

lemma 8.12. assume  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$  and  $\left\| \log(w^{\mathrm{new}}) - \log(w^{\mathrm{mid}}) \right\|_{\infty} \leq 0.01$ . then we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \| \alpha_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| \beta_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = o (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}} ^ {2}) \cdot \left\| \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. we recall the definition of vector  $\alpha_{i,s,t} \in \mathbb{r}^m$  and  $\beta_{i,s,t} \in \mathbb{r}^m$  from definition 8.3 as follows:

$$
\alpha_ {i, s, t} = \sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}} \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

$$
\beta_ {i, s, t} = \sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}} \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) \cdot q (z _ {t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

where recall that  $y_{s,t} = z_t + s(x_t - z_t)$  and  $y_{s,t} = z_t + s(x_t - z_t)$ . recall from definition 3.2 that  $p(v) = v^{1/2} a (a^\top va)^{-1} a^\top v^{1/2} = v^{1/2} \cdot q(v) \cdot v^{1/2}$ . we therefore can rewrite  $\alpha_{i,s,t}$  and  $\beta_{i,s,t}$  as

$$
\alpha_ {i, s, t} = \frac {\sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} e _ {i},
$$

$$
\beta_ {i, s, t} = \frac {\sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t} y _ {s , t}}} p (z _ {t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t}}} e _ {i}.
$$

our assumptions  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$  and  $\left\| \log(w^{\mathrm{new}}) - \log(w^{\mathrm{mid}})\right\|_{\infty} \leq 0.01$  imply that

$$
\| \log (y _ {s, t}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) \| _ {\infty} \leq 0. 1 \qquad \mathrm {a n d} \qquad \| \log (z _ {t}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) \| _ {\infty} \leq 0. 1.
$$

therefore we can upper bound  $\alpha_{i,s,t}$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max _ {i, s, t} \| \alpha_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \max _ {i, s, t} \left\| \frac {\sqrt {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} e _ {i} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \max _ {i, s, t} \left\| y _ {s, t} ^ {- 1} (z _ {t} - x _ {t}) \right\| _ {\infty} \cdot \left\| \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} e _ {i} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $\beta_{i,s,t}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \beta_ {i, s, t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} e _ {i} ^ {\top} \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t}}} p (z _ {t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t} y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t} y _ {s , t}}} p (z _ {t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t}}} e _ {i} \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left[ \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t}}} p (z _ {t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t} y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t} y _ {s , t}}} p (z _ {t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t}}} \right] \\ = \mathrm {t r} [ \sqrt {d} p (z _ {t}) b p (y _ {s, t}) c p (y _ {s, t}) b p (z _ {t}) \sqrt {d} ], \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we define diagonal matrices

$$
b = \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{\sqrt {z _ {t} y _ {s , t}}}, c = \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}}, d = \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{z _ {t}}.
$$

thus, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {t r} [ \sqrt {d} p (z _ {t}) b p (y _ {s, t}) c p (y _ {s, t}) b p (z _ {t}) \sqrt {d} ] \\ \leq \| c \| _ {\infty} \cdot \operatorname {t r} [ \sqrt {d} p (z _ {t}) b ^ {2} p (z _ {t}) \sqrt {d} ] \quad p (y _ {s, t}) \cdot c \cdot p (y _ {s, t}) \preceq \| c \| _ {\infty} \cdot i \\ = \| c \| _ {\infty} \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ b p \left(z _ {t}\right) d p \left(z _ {t}\right) b \right] \\ = o (1) \cdot \| c \| _ {\infty} \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ b ^ {2} \right] \quad p \left(z _ {t}\right) \cdot d \cdot p \left(z _ {t}\right) \preceq o (1) \cdot i \\ = o (1) \cdot \| c \| _ {\infty} \cdot \| b \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}}\right) \cdot \left\| \log \left(w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}\right) - \log \left(w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/98db5ecba30a12d3e3efa7b822fdd07c4077350976064cc08eb7bd27cc808016.jpg)

# 8.4.3 upper bounding  $\sum_{i=1}^{m} \|\gamma_i\|_2^4$

lemma 8.13. assume  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{comp}} \leq 0.01$  and  $\left\| \log(w^{\mathrm{new}}) - \log(w^{\mathrm{mid}}) \right\|_{\infty} \leq 0.01$ . then for any  $s \in s$  and  $t \in \mathcal{t}$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \| \gamma_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {4} = o (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}} ^ {4}) \cdot \left\| \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. we recall the definition of vector  $\gamma_{i,s,t} \in \mathbb{r}^m$  from definition 8.3 as follows:

$$
\gamma_ {i, s, t} = \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot (z _ {t} - x _ {t}) \cdot q (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot e _ {i},
$$

where recall that  $y_{s,t} = (z_t + s(x_t - z_t))$  and  $y_{s,t} = (z_t + s(x_t - z_t))$ . recall from definition 3.2 that  $p(v) = v^{1/2} a(a^\top va)^{-1} a^\top v^{1/2} = v^{1/2} \cdot q(v) \cdot v^{1/2}$ . we therefore can rewrite  $\gamma_{i,s,t}$  as

$$
\gamma_ {i, s, t} = \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} \cdot p (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} \cdot p (y _ {s, t}) \cdot \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} \cdot e _ {i}.
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \gamma_ {i, s, t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {4} \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left(e _ {i} ^ {\top} \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} e _ {i}\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ = \mathrm {t r} \left[ \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} \right] \\ \cdot \left. \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}} p (y _ {s, t}) \frac {\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}}{\sqrt {y _ {s , t}}} \right] \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left[ \left(\sqrt {d} p c p b p c p \sqrt {d}\right) ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we define diagonal matrices  $b, c, d \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times m}$ ,

$$
b = \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}} - w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{y _ {s , t}}, c = \frac {z _ {t} - x _ {t}}{y _ {s , t}}, d = \frac {w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}{y _ {s , t}}.
$$

and for simplicity, we use  $p$  to denote  $p(y_{s,t})$ . by our assumptions that  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{simp}} \leq 0.01$  and

$$
\left\| \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}) \right\| _ {\infty} \leq 0. 0 1,
$$

we have

$$
\left\| \log (y _ {s, t}) - \log (w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) \right\| _ {\infty} \leq 0. 1.
$$

therefore we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {t r} \left[ (\sqrt {d} p c p b p c p \sqrt {d}) ^ {2} \right] \\ = \operatorname {t r} [ (\sqrt {b} p c p d p c p \sqrt {b}) ^ {2} ] \\ \leq o (1) \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ \left(\sqrt {b} p c ^ {2} p \sqrt {b}\right) ^ {2} \right] \quad p \cdot d \cdot p \preceq o (1) \cdot i \\ \leq o (1) \cdot \| c \| _ {\infty} ^ {4} \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ b ^ {2} \right] \quad p \cdot c ^ {2} \cdot p \preceq \| c \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \cdot i \\ = o (1) \cdot \| c \| _ {\infty} ^ {4} \cdot \| b \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {c o m p}} ^ {4}\right) \cdot \left\| \log \left(w ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}\right) - \log \left(w ^ {\mathrm {m i d}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/f3eb511d1efe68c761a79a2e3cbaba3208ec0acb9866b6520ac4e64717ae4356.jpg)

# 8.5 variance upper bound for random gaussian matrices

<table><tr><td>notation</td><td>statement</td><td>quantity</td><td>upper bound</td></tr><tr><td>η^tη</td><td>lemma 8.15</td><td>var[ση^tη - ση^trη]</td><td>o(1/r) · σ||ηi,s||24</td></tr><tr><td>α^tβ</td><td>lemma 8.16</td><td>var[σα^tβ - σβ^trrα]</td><td>o(1/r) · σ||βi,s,t||22||αi,s,t||22</td></tr><tr><td>γ^tγ</td><td>lemma 8.17</td><td>var[σγ^tγ - σγ^trγ]</td><td>o(1/r) · σ||γi,s,t||24</td></tr></table>

table 7: variance upper bounds for  $\eta^{\top}\eta$ ,  $\alpha^{\top}\beta$  and  $\gamma^{\top}\gamma$ , where  $\eta$ ,  $\alpha$ ,  $\beta$  and  $\gamma$  are defined in definition 8.3.

our variance bounds for the terms where we applied jl matrices are summarized in table 7. these results are consequences of the following lemma 8.14.

# 8.5.1 a variance bound for random gaussian matrices

lemma 8.14 (jl variance bound). given vectors  $x, y \in \mathbb{r}^m$ . let  $r \in \mathbb{r}^{r \times m}$  be a random gaussian matrix with  $r_{i,j} \sim \mathcal{n}(0,1 / r)$ . then we have

$$
\mathbf {e} _ {r} [ x ^ {\top} r ^ {\top} r y ] = x ^ {\top} y, \qquad \mathbf {v a r} _ {r} [ x ^ {\top} r ^ {\top} r y ] \leq \frac {3}{r} \cdot \| x \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| y \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. since  $\mathbf{e}[r^\top r] = i$ , we have

$$
\mathbf {\delta} _ {r} [ x ^ {\top} r ^ {\top} r y ] = x ^ {\top} y.
$$

next we prove the bound on the variance. we use  $r_{i}$  for each  $i \in [r]$  to denote the column vector that corresponds to the  $i$ th row of  $r$ . we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {v a r} _ {r} [ x ^ {\top} r ^ {\top} r y ] = \mathbf {v a r} _ {r} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ r ]} x ^ {\top} r _ {i} r _ {i} ^ {\top} y \right] \\ = r \mathbf {v} _ {r _ {1}} \left[ x ^ {\top} r _ {1} r _ {1} ^ {\top} y \right] \\ \leq r \underset {r _ {1}} {\mathbf {e}} [ (x ^ {\top} r _ {1} r _ {1} ^ {\top} y) ^ {2} ] \\ \leq r \left(\mathbf {e} _ {r _ {1}} [ (x ^ {\top} r _ {1}) ^ {4} ]\right) ^ {1 / 2} \cdot \left(\mathbf {e} _ {r _ {1}} [ (y ^ {\top} r _ {1}) ^ {4} ]\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq \frac {3}{r} \cdot \| x \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| y \| _ {2} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality is because  $x^{\top}r_{1}$  is a gaussian random variable with variance  $\| x\| _2^2 /r$ .

![](images/fa76c63998c21f3414c4615794b84b45393790fa154980d4b2e84a982742f81c.jpg)

# 8.5.2 variance bound for  $\eta^{\top}\eta$ ,  $\alpha^{\top}\beta$  and  $\gamma^{\top}\gamma$

the lemmas below all follow immediately from lemma 8.14.

lemma 8.15 (variance bound for  $\eta^\top \eta$ ). let  $\sigma_{i,\mathrm{dis}}$  and  $\sigma_{i,\mathrm{jl}}$  be defined as follows

$$
\sigma_ {i, \mathrm {d i s}} = \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {s} \eta_ {i, s} ^ {\top} \eta_ {i, s}, \quad \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} = \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {s} \eta_ {i, s} ^ {\top} r _ {\eta , s} ^ {\top} r _ {\eta , s} \eta_ {i, s}.
$$

where  $r_{\eta ,s}\in \mathbb{r}^{r\times m}$  for  $s\in s$  are independent random gaussian matrices with  $(r_{\eta ,s})_{i,j}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,1 / r)$ . then, we have

$$
\frac {\mathbf {e}}{r} [ \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} ] = \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {d i s}}, \qquad \mathbf {v a r} _ {r} [ \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} ] \leq \frac {3}{r} \cdot \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {s} ^ {2} \| \eta_ {i, s} \| _ {2} ^ {4}.
$$

lemma 8.16 (variance bound for  $\alpha^{\top}\beta$ ). let  $\sigma_{i,\mathrm{dis}}$  and  $\sigma_{i,\mathrm{jl}}$  be defined as follows

$$
\sigma_ {i, \mathrm {d i s}} = \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} \omega_ {s} \beta_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \alpha_ {i, s, t}, \quad \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} = \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} \omega_ {s} \beta_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\alpha \beta , s, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\alpha \beta , s, t} \alpha_ {i, s, t}.
$$

where  $r_{\alpha \beta, s, t} \in \mathbb{r}^{r \times m}$  for  $s \in s, t \in \mathcal{t}$  are independent random gaussian matrices with  $(r_{\alpha \beta, s, t})_{i,j} \sim \mathcal{n}(0, 1 / r)$ . then, we have

$$
\frac {\mathbf {e}}{r} [ \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} ] = \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {d i s}}, \qquad \mathbf {v a r} _ {r} [ \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} ] \leq \frac {3}{r} \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} ^ {2} \omega_ {s} ^ {2} \| \beta_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| \alpha_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

lemma 8.17 (variance bound for  $\gamma^{\top}\gamma$ ). let  $\sigma_{i,\mathrm{dis}}$  and  $\sigma_{i,\mathrm{jl}}$  be defined as follows

$$
\sigma_ {i, \mathrm {d i s}} = \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \sum_ {s ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} \omega_ {s} \omega_ {s ^ {\prime}} \gamma_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} \gamma_ {i, s ^ {\prime}, t}, \quad \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} = \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \sum_ {s ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} \omega_ {s} \omega_ {s ^ {\prime}} \gamma_ {i, s, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\gamma , s, s ^ {\prime}, t} ^ {\top} r _ {\gamma , s, s ^ {\prime}, t} \gamma_ {i, s ^ {\prime}, t}.
$$

where  $r_{\gamma, s, s', t} \in \mathbb{r}^{r \times m}$  are independent random gaussian matrices with  $(r_{\gamma, s, s', t})_{i,j} \sim \mathcal{n}(0, 1/r)$ . then, we have

$$
\underset {r} {\mathbf {e}} [ \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} ] = \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {d i s}}, \qquad \underset {r} {\mathbf {v a r}} [ \sigma_ {i, \mathrm {j l}} ] \leq \frac {3}{r} \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {t}} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \sum_ {s ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {s}} \omega_ {t} ^ {2} \omega_ {s} ^ {2} \omega_ {s ^ {\prime}} ^ {2} \| \gamma_ {i, s, t} \| _ {2} ^ {4}.
$$

<table><tr><td>notation</td><td>statement</td><td>quantity</td><td>upper bound</td></tr><tr><td>η^1η</td><td>lemma 8.18</td><td>|∫η^1η - ∑η^1η|</td><td>o(poly(n)/2^{2n})</td></tr><tr><td>θ^1θ</td><td>lemma 8.20</td><td>|∫θ^1θ - ∑θ^1θ|</td><td>o(poly(n)/2^{2n})</td></tr><tr><td>α^1β</td><td>lemma 8.21</td><td>|∫α^1β - ∑α^1β|</td><td>o(poly(n)/2^{2n})</td></tr><tr><td>γ^1γ</td><td>lemma 8.22</td><td>|∫γ^1γ - ∑γ^1γ|</td><td>o(poly(n)/2^{2n})</td></tr></table>
